# Findings register

Every row is a hypothesis with the evidence it rested on, never a closed door. A later reader may confirm it, challenge it or mark it unable to verify. Overturning a row succeeds when the overturn is supported by evidence or reasoning, including a counterexample derived from unchanged sources; it names the row and what it rests on. Reconsideration needs no changed source. This file is tier `history`: it justifies, it never builds. No row here outranks a `SPEC`; where a row and its home disagree, the home wins and the row is the defect. Rows carry attributed design reasoning only: no scores, tallies, readiness labels or execution plans.

To add a row: take the next free `F-nn`. Give it a date, a source, a one-line finding, and a verdict from the closed set — accepted / rejected / deferred / overturned / unverified. Evidence must be something a clone can resolve (a repository path in backticks); where the source is working material absent from clones, say so as "working material, absent from clones: <path>" — written bare, since it is not a path a clone can resolve. Name what evidence or reasoning would change the row; when the evidence cell already names the re-check that would overturn it (a re-read of the cited source, a re-derivation against the tracked tree), this cell may be left blank rather than repeat it. Leave the re-verification cell empty until someone re-checks the row, then record who, when, and the result.

Mapped quotations retain their source-map attribution; primary comparisons below distinguish pinned-before from working candidate. | Mapped diagnoses retained: S13 [missing-contract]: Ruleset consistency reads stored rules, not the actual future-booking blocker set. Persist and resume the candidate while current rules stay effective; recheck immediately before apply. Scripted/end-state; see mechanism comparisons and existing BUILD obligations. S14b [missing-contract]: Guest own-booking management exists; its console, independent request identities, both monthly limits and user-chosen cancellation effects need the new composition contracts. Scripted/end-state; see mechanism comparisons and existing BUILD obligations. S14c [contradiction]: Accepted changes to an already-booked quote conflict with forever/forward-only wording. Add explicit versioned re-agreement, keeping one booking/history and atomic participant draws. Scripted/end-state; see mechanism comparisons and existing BUILD obligations. MT-59 [later-proof]: Bobby moves13:00→15:30, including failed/stale moves and a late old clock firing.. Later implementation/composition proof, with listed prerequisite contracts: Owner reminder authorization, rescheduling and truthful delivery. Existing D28 proves a kind reminder with a grant. Compare MT-58–60: owner rather than assumed student destination, atomic configuration activation and obsolete move/cancel firings are additional composition contracts, not a new notification subsystem. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. MT-76 [missing-contract]: Arthur’s Saturday request is booked at 10:00–10:45 and moved to 10:15–11:00; a separate Sunday request succeeds, a separate Wednesday request refuses COUNT, Saturday is kept then cancelled before start, and Wednesday is retried.. Missing contract: Per-participant monthly count/time admission and lifecycle. Current quota has rate/window/scope and Q4 automatically restores a cancelled draw. Compare HR-22, MT-52/55/76/88: participant-wide aggregation and distinct user-chosen cancellation outcomes need an explicit carrier and evaluation contract. Q4 remains valid as a Release allowance fixture, not universal policy. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. MT-78 [missing-contract]: Owner cancels a personal Event or lesson, removes a reminder policy, or tries to remove a governing rule.. Missing contract: General field and rule lifecycle with dependent removal. Compare the attribute_schema placeholder and no-delete law with MT-66/67 and CRUD-11/12: the schema vocabulary does not specify saved field retirement, type changes or dependent calculations. Draft-row deletion does not establish saved-schema mutation. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. MT-88 [missing-contract]: Run the October group create/race/Saturdaycancel/participantremove-rejoin sequence.. Missing contract: Per-participant monthly count/time admission and lifecycle. Current quota has rate/window/scope and Q4 automatically restores a cancelled draw. Compare HR-22, MT-52/55/76/88: participant-wide aggregation and distinct user-chosen cancellation outcomes need an explicit carrier and evaluation contract. Q4 remains valid as a Release allowance fixture, not universal policy. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. CRUD-16 [later-proof]: Owner-entered booking — owner. Later implementation/composition proof, with listed prerequisite contracts: Atomic commits, retry identity and same-booking moves. Existing A1/A4 and H9 already cover one winner, stable write-id replay and atomic move. The story is additional composition proof; only new pre-response entry identities and new transactional participants require contract propagation. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. CRUD-17 [contradiction]: Guest's own booking — scoped guest. Contradiction requiring approved propagation: Bounded guest conversation and credential-derived context. Compare the explicit no-agent source/scenario with HR-18 and S11. This is approved behavior requiring coordinated contract repair; H8/H9 own-booking management already exists but does not establish a guest console. No new unrestricted chatbot follows. Status applies to the named obligation, not every subfeature of the mechanism; scripted end-state only. CRUD-26 [missing-contract]: Shared request, participant set and coordination rights — authorized actors. Missing contract: Reusable groups, shared requests and booking participants. Existing Shared publication and cross-owner one-commitment offer are not a stored reusable group plus accountless group request lifecycle. Compare HR-30/S13d with those distinct objects; do not substitute four on-app owner offers for four accountless recipient links.
